# NHL Blender — completed grade, audit, patch continuation

Updated November 5, 2026. All slate dates and source/start times use America/New_York.

**The five remaining selected slates are now adjudicated and graded: October 13, October 24, November 1, November 2 and November 3.** Each has a full-slate/both-direction G0 review, the complete projected pools for the three selected attack sides, separate G0–G18 decisions including G10.5, fixed replay names, official scorer results, a mechanism audit and a correction carried to the following replay. The final execution patch is below.

These are historical reconstructions conducted on November 5. Results were exposed during research; none is represented as an independent blind test or an originally issued pregame card. Original assistant picks, later replacements and retrospective selections remain separate. A scorer hit never retroactively certifies every gate.

**Grading rule:** 3/3 is WIN; any lower Core 3 result is LOSS. Gauthier’s three goals on November 3 are one scorer hit, not three successful legs. Empty-net and credited own-goal outcomes count for official scorer results; shootout conversions do not.

September 29 is a correction of issued versions, not a new replay card. September 30 retains the prior diagnostic’s stated uncertainty rather than being relabeled a recovered historical G18 lock. The complete October 1 work is retained later in this report. The five new applications do not rewrite either baseline.

## Execution patch carried into the daily Blender

Execution corrections inside the existing NHL G0–G18 gates; no added gates, numerical scores, thresholds or replacement rulebook.

| Existing gates | Applied execution correction |
|---|---|
| G0 / G3 / G5 | Screen the full eligible slate and both goalie directions before advancing three games/attack sides. Inspect the actual area inside each broad danger band and its league-relative performance; preserve stronger areas and current counterevidence. A rink map does not establish glove/blocker/five-hole weakness. Do not carry a prior winning recipient across a different goalie map. |
| G2 / G6 / G17 | Use the full dated eligible pool, including depth forwards, all defensemen, 11F/7D alternatives and returns/recalls. Availability controls over stale odds/lineup labels. Separate goalie confirmation, dressed availability, EV deployment and PP assignments; a confirmed goalie does not confirm every unit. Reopen the full pregame comparison on material changes while preserving the projected version. |
| G6 / G10 / G10.5 / G12 | Keep EV5v5, EV4v4, EV3v3, PP5v4, PP5v3, PP4v3, PK and extra-attacker access distinct. Establish finisher versus creator within each state. A player may create in one event and finish in another. An EV feed is not proof of the same PP feed, and PP1 does not outrank an evidenced PP2/EV/PK route. |
| G7 / G11 | Use actual dated prior appearances and current shot locations/types, with injury/scratch gaps explicit. A multi-goal game is one recurrence date. Last five player appearances can differ from the team's last five games; missed games are not zero-shot games. Separate prior-season history, preseason, current conversion and current mechanism. |
| G9 / G10.5 / G12 / G16 | Show Primary, meaningful Challengers and Decision using the complete pool. Compare each finalist's latest physical route, actual access and competing event owners. Preserve saved own chances, rebounds/tips, creators and eventual receivers as different evidence. A hot sequence, zero-shot game, line number or PP label alone neither advances nor kills a player. |
| G8 | Separate bookmaker prices, model probabilities, point/assist/shot markets and settled historical logs. Do not invent opener/close movement, treat cross-book snapshots as movement or order recipients by odds. Search additional sources when needed; unavailable historical movement remains labeled and does not block the run. |
| G13 | Review each finalist's qualitative name/date/jersey/birth-date alignment and verified real milestones. Distinguish one-event targets from distant and already-achieved marks, including franchise milestones. No invented gematria total, formula, weighting or stopping requirement. Continue through G14–G18. |
| G14 / G15 / G16 | WHO requires a documented anomaly plus a physical route and real opportunity; it is not a quota or a forced contrarian name. Support players retain independent finishing routes. Preserve negative and competing evidence through final recipient separation. |
| G17 / G18 | Apply an individual pregame cutoff to every game and source. After-start articles are later corroboration, not pregame proof. Preserve original, replacement, conflicting and late versions; never retrofit a started leg or insert an eventual scorer into the earlier card. A later confirmation can trigger a fresh eligible pregame rerun, not a cosmetic label change. |
| G18 / postgame audit | Grade official credited goals: 3/3 is WIN, anything less LOSS; count empty-net and credited own-goal outcomes as scorer hits but exclude shootout conversions. Audit goalie target, location, event type, strength state and recipient separately. Empty nets, defender own goals, boards/skate bounces and goals against a replacement goalie do not automatically validate the selected goalie mechanism. |
| G10 / G12 / postgame audit | When shot type is missing or coordinates conflict with the narrative, inspect the official play report, game sequence and an independent recap. Do not infer a snap, rebound or creator chain from coordinates alone. Report partial component matches honestly. Prior feed examples establish access and role, not a new requirement that identical assistants must recur. |
| All gates | Keep research, adjudication, original published cards and retrospective diagnostics distinct. Never equate populated journal rows with completed gate work or use retrospective outcomes as original/blind results. Produce the actual gate-by-gate decision without app development, new fail-safes, invented weights or skipped gates. |

Application recorded 2026-11-05 05:53:13 PM EST. Applied to the daily projected/confirmed execution instructions and the next-run continuation record. Existing issued cards, game decisions and start cutoffs are preserved. This patch does not by itself certify a new projected/confirmed Core 3; the next eligible evaluation must apply the actual corrected gates before publishing.

## How the evidence is read

NHL EDGE figures are the completed 2025–26 season, kept separate from 2026–27 game logs and dated current play events. H/M/L means vendor high-danger/midrange/long-range shots and goals. Those bands overlap conceptually with, but are not added to, the separate 17-area location map. C / LS / HS / LC / RC / CP means crease / low slot / high slot / left circle / right circle / center point. Percentiles compare the same area with the league; raw crease and circle save percentages are not directly ranked against one another.

All current player/goalie games are filtered strictly before the replay date. Player last-five appearances keep actual dates and can extend beyond the team’s last five games. A missed appearance is not a zero-shot performance. SO and goalie-free events are excluded from live-goalie mechanism recurrence; extra-attacker situations remain separate.

A play coordinate and approximate distance do not establish shot height, glove/blocker/five-hole, screen quality or a rebound. Missing shot types and unusual coordinates receive narrative/official-report checks. Identical assistants are not required to recur: ownership, area, event type, strength and actual creators are shown separately so partial support does not become an invented exact sequence.

Starter confirmations below are dated archived DFO reports linked to their named source. Where the underlying beat post could not be independently reopened, the status means DFO-reported CONFIRMED, not an independently authenticated team confirmation. Skater and PP roles remain projected unless separately established. A later archived confirmation is a pregame update for that game, not an earlier full-slate delivered card. No game-day result enters the reconstructed pregame statistical inputs.

### CGY-ANA — Beckett Sennecke

**G0 — Game / goalie target**

ANA attacks Dustin Wolf in CGY-ANA. Wolf allowed 16 goals on 87 shots in three prior starts. The most recent meeting already exposed near-net receiving and a low-slot activation; this is stronger evidence than treating every Anaheim perimeter attempt as equivalent.

**G1 — Environment**

Anaheim is home for the rematch after winning the October 10 meeting. Gauthier and Poehling are unavailable; their vacated access changes the pool and units rather than authorizing their insertion from a market page.

**G2 — Full player pool**

All 18 dated projected skaters are retained: Alex Killorn, Leo Carlsson, Beckett Sennecke, Nikita Nesterenko, Mikael Granlund, Sam Colangelo, Frank Vatrano, Roger McQueen, A.J. Greer, Jeff Malott, Tim Washe, Judd Caulfield, Jackson LaCombe, Noah Warren, Pavel Mintyukov, Drew Helleson, Tyson Hinds, Tristan Luneau. The comparison table below preserves PP2, depth and defensemen.

**G3 — Opponent attack map**

Prior-season NHL EDGE area map: Center Point 0.963636 (percentile 67.8); Crease 0.692308 (percentile 23.7); High Slot 0.860465 (percentile 55.9); L Circle 0.927273 (percentile 74.6); Low Slot 0.806789 (percentile 37.3); R Circle 0.909639 (percentile 62.7). The crease/low-slot and left-net-side branches are the relevant targets. Wolf is comparatively stronger in the circles, high slot and point; broad poor recent save percentage does not erase those differences.

**G4 — Damage mechanism**

Beckett Sennecke prior NHL EDGE bands: high: 88 shots / 14 goals; mid: 59 shots / 6 goals; long: 23 shots / 0 goals. Sennecke had 14 crease and 74 low-slot attempts. Carlsson had 62 low-slot but no recorded crease attempts; Killorn 6/53; Granlund 8/32. Caulfield, Colangelo, Washe and Nesterenko have current close attempts despite smaller or unavailable older samples.

**G5 — Exact matchup alignment**

The October 7 eight-foot snap and 7.3-foot tip align directly with the interior target. His October 10 attempts from 31 and 41 feet are counterevidence against assuming all current volume remains inside.

**G6 — Opportunity / access**

Projected L1 with Killorn/Carlsson and PP1 with Granlund, Carlsson, LaCombe and Judd Caulfield. The separate PP2 Killorn/Colangelo/Vatrano/McQueen/Luneau branch remains eligible. Gauthier is excluded by availability.

**G7 — Recurrence / sequence**

2026-10-10: 2 shots, 0 goals; 2026-10-09: 0 shots, 0 goals; 2026-10-07: 7 shots, 2 goals; 2026-10-04: 3 shots, 0 goals; 2026-10-02: 1 shots, 1 goals. The two close PP goals belong to one game, not two separate games. There is an additional October 2 EV inside wrist goal. October 4 4v3 and October 7 6v3 attempts are identified separately from ordinary 5v4.

**G8 — Market fingerprint**

A dated October 13 iHeart preview lists Sennecke points O0.5 -175 and assists O0.5 +135. These are points/assist quotes, not an anytime-goal price or authenticated movement. ### Patch leaving this application

- A matching rink location does not validate the wrong strength or creator chain; classify scorer, location, state and mechanism separately.
- Repeated goals in one game are one deployment occasion; retain later location retreat and limited minutes as active separation evidence.
- Re-evaluate current recipient competition across lines and units; creator identity does not fix which teammate receives the next chance.
- A return from suspension or injury changes opportunity, even where projected PP labels remain. Do not invent current recurrence for the returnee.

### Patch leaving this application

- Reopen receiving ownership after center/creator availability changes, preserving the competing independent receivers.
- A player can be a close finisher on one date and a feeder on another; recent goals do not freeze his role.
- A goal with no shot type needs sequence/narrative verification; do not convert a nearby coordinate into a snap or passing chain.
- Returning players’ last five appearances may predate the team’s last five games; show the actual dates and inspect those games without treating missed games as zero shots.

Greer survives the EV receiving branch. Sennecke’s closer PP recurrence, Gauthier’s ten-shot latest appearance and Caulfield’s net-front bat are genuine counterevidence. No WHO quota or avoidance of popular players decides the choice.

**G17 — Contradiction audit**

Johansson’s dated confirmation is separate from projected lines. Mintyukov’s return changes the defense pool, Terry remains out, and Greer has no projected PP. Sennecke’s 6v5 finish and Luneau’s 6v5 snap are not counted as normal 5v4 evidence.

**G18 — Final lock**

Fixed retrospective selection: A.J. Greer — WHO, OTT-ANA. Goal count and mechanism are graded against this name without substituting a later scorer.

### Patch leaving this application

- At recipient separation explicitly compare each finalist’s latest own release location, current line access and state against the competing receivers; a depth anomaly does not outrank a better-current physical route.
- Keep activation, location, owner, strength state, shot type and creator chain as separate audit dimensions; partial matches do not become exact-mechanism wins.
- Early games have individual source cutoffs. After-start PP articles are corroboration only; use independently dated prior events and pregame reports for the run.
- If a starter is relieved, subsequent goals against the backup cannot validate the original goalie target.
- Preserve later actual deployment changes as postgame audit facts rather than silently rewriting the captured projected pool.

2026-10-31: 2 shots, 1 goals; 2026-10-29: 2 shots, 0 goals; 2026-10-27: 1 shots, 0 goals; 2026-10-24: 1 shots, 1 goals; 2026-10-22: 1 shots, 0 goals. The close EV finish on October 31 and PP backhand on October 24 are different dates/states. Intervening attempts were farther out. This is not a claim of five straight close-goal games.

**G8 — Market fingerprint**

Dated Calgary–San Jose previews and Misa market searches were checked; no authenticated Misa goal-price movement was recovered. A missing historical quote is labeled and the full physical comparison proceeds.

**G9 — Player × environment**

Sherwood and defense support can create a near-net Misa touch while Marchment draws inside coverage. Smith/Celebrini on the first line have independent close routes and are directly compared.

**G10 — Event route**

Selected EV chain: Trouba/Sherwood support Misa’s own crease wrist. Earlier PP: Toffoli/Trouba support Misa’s backhand. Current PP2 is treated as access with a changed creator, not a confirmed replay of that old feed.

**G10.5 — Recipient separation**

Primary: Celebrini. Challengers: Misa, Smith, Marchment, Toffoli, Sherwood, Graf, Gaudette, Trouba, Allan and all other skaters. Decision: Misa owns the most exact current crease wrist branch; Celebrini’s latest four-foot PP backhand attempt and Smith’s 13-foot EV finish remain genuine alternative owners.

**G11 — Continuation**

Misa/Sherwood remain together at EV, retaining the verified close receiving chain. Gaudette’s return and Toffoli’s separate PP1 role change the secondary PP support and are preserved as limitations on PP continuity.

**G12 — Event ownership**

Misa owns his wrist/backhand, while Sherwood can own a PK snap and Trouba his activation. A creator in one Misa goal remains a finisher in another state or chance.

**G13 — Numerology / alignment**

Michael Misa; slate 2026-11-02; birth date 2007-02-16; historical jersey 77; pregame NHL regular-season totals 60 GP, 12 G, 16 A, 28 P. Review November 2, Michael/Misa identity, jersey 77 and February 16 birth date qualitatively. Twelve goals/28 points are achieved totals, not a ten-goal target. Smith’s 48 goals puts 50 two goals away; Misa’s name/jersey alignment is not converted into an invented gematria score.

**G14 — WHO / anomaly**

L2/PP2 rookie-age receiver with actual crease finishing is the WHO anomaly. The choice requires its own physical route and observed access; youth, obscurity or lack of PP1 alone do not qualify him.

**G15 — Finisher protection**

Sherwood/Marchment support EV; Trouba/Orlov/Gaudette provide a separate projected PP2 branch. Other close receivers remain independent rather than being permanently assigned facilitation.

**G16 — Final competition**

Misa survives the exact crease-owning comparison. Smith’s larger current shot sequence and Celebrini’s close PP attempt are the strongest challenges. Wolf’s improved latest start remains counterevidence rather than an omitted negative.

**G17 — Contradiction audit**

Wolf has a dated confirmation. The pool excludes scratched Dellandrea and retains Gaudette. PP2 differs from the old scoring example; no Toffoli-to-Misa current feed is falsely confirmed. SO/EN events remain separate.

**G18 — Final lock**

Fixed retrospective selection: Michael Misa — WHO, CGY-SJS. Goal count and mechanism are graded against this name without substituting a later scorer.

### Patch leaving this application

- Empty-net goals count for official scorer grading but never validate the selected goalie-exploitation mechanism.
- Shootout conversions remain excluded from official scorer and live-goalie route grades.
- Differentiate chance-generation success, saved own attempt, rebound/next-touch receiver and eventual goal owner; do not grant goal credit for correct access or an assist.
- Confirmed-input reruns must refresh the entire eligible pool when a returning/called-up player changes deployment, preserving the projected version rather than rewriting it after the result.

### ANA-VAN — Cutter Gauthier

**G0 — Game / goalie target**

ANA attacks Kevin Lankinen in ANA-VAN. Lankinen’s weak high slot, low slot and right circle advance Anaheim. His crease performance is comparatively stronger, so the previous slate’s closest net-front winner is not automatically promoted against this different goalie map.

**G1 — Environment**

Anaheim is away, with Greer injured and Myatovic recalled. The top two projected lines remain intact; Myatovic’s debut opportunity is evaluated without invented prior NHL tracking.

**G2 — Full player pool**

All 18 dated projected skaters are retained: Alex Killorn, Leo Carlsson, Judd Caulfield, Cutter Gauthier, Mikael Granlund, Beckett Sennecke, Nico Myatovic, Tim Washe, Sam Colangelo, Jeff Malott, Roger McQueen, Nikita Nesterenko, Jackson LaCombe, Drew Helleson, Pavel Mintyukov, Noah Warren, Travis Mitchell, Tristan Luneau. The comparison table below preserves PP2, depth and defensemen.

**G3 — Opponent attack map**

Prior-season NHL EDGE area map: Center Point 0.936842 (percentile 20.3); Crease 0.78125 (percentile 59.3); High Slot 0.785047 (percentile 5.1); L Circle 0.934426 (percentile 79.7); Low Slot 0.783626 (percentile 10.2); R Circle 0.850746 (percentile 10.2). High slot is at the 5.1st percentile, low slot/right circle 10.2; crease is 59.3. This is an inside/right-circle shooting target rather than a claim that the crease is Lankinen’s weakest area.

**G4 — Damage mechanism**

Cutter Gauthier prior NHL EDGE bands: high: 52 shots / 13 goals; mid: 117 shots / 18 goals; long: 37 shots / 2 goals. Gauthier’s older 117 midrange shots/18 goals support an own-release mechanism; his 52 high-danger shots/13 goals add inside range. Caulfield and Sennecke’s close tips are real alternatives but are not identical to the selected high-slot/circle release.

**G5 — Exact matchup alignment**

Current own PP slaps from 24.1/35.5 feet and the latest 18.4-foot 5v3 wrist/19.2-foot 5v4 snap demonstrate actual receiving releases. The unassisted 12.1-foot EV wrist is independent; five recent goals are decomposed rather than used as a heater shortcut.

**G6 — Opportunity / access**

Projected L2 with Granlund/Sennecke and PP1 with Carlsson/LaCombe. Caulfield is L1/PP2; McQueen/Nesterenko/Luneau have a separate PP2 route. The card does not copy PP1 access onto the new third-line recall.

**G7 — Recurrence / sequence**

2026-11-01: 5 shots, 3 goals; 2026-10-29: 10 shots, 2 goals; 2026-10-27: 4 shots, 0 goals; 2026-10-24: 4 shots, 0 goals; 2026-10-22: 2 shots, 0 goals. **G9 — Player × environment**

Carlsson/LaCombe/Granlund can feed Gauthier into the weaker inside/circle release area. A Caulfield/Sennecke crease tip is another owner and attacks a comparatively less weak area in this specific map.

### Patch leaving this application

- Do not carry a winning recipient across different goalie maps: Lankinen’s high-slot/right-circle weakness differs from his stronger crease record.
- A later scoring event can preserve owner/location/state while changing shot subtype or creators; report those components without claiming an exact repeated pass sequence.
- Historical assisted-chain examples establish role/access evidence, not a new rule that the identical assistants must recur.
- Keep simultaneous creator and finisher routes open until G10.5/G12 comparison; the selected name can assist a teammate rather than finish.
- The original November 3 Kaprizov/Guentzel/Jankowski trio stays 0/3. This new retrospective 2/3 is a separate diagnostic card, not a replacement win.

## Game-by-game G0–G18 decisions

### PHI @ NJD — Luke Hughes

**G0 — Game / goalie target**

New Jersey attacks Joseph Woll. The detailed map changes the earlier aggregate screen: Woll's high-slot save rate was .789 (6.8th percentile), while his crease, low-slot and long-range bands were much less vulnerable. This replaces the initial Florida branch before this replay card was recorded.

**G1 — Environment**

New Jersey opens at home. Philadelphia travels after its September 30 opener. In four completed preseason games Philadelphia conceded 5, 5, 0 and 5 goals; this is supporting context, not a scorer-selection rule.

**G2 — Full player pool**

All 18 projected Devils remain eligible. Mercer is retained under his October 1 New Jersey identity despite the current roster listing him elsewhere. Lombardi uses actual preseason evidence rather than fabricated NHL tracking. Both defense units and depth forwards remain in the comparison.

**G3 — Opponent attack map**

Target the high slot, not a blanket weak-goalie label: Woll .789 in the high slot versus .978 at center point, .775 at the crease and .818 in the low slot. The NHL percentiles place high slot at 6.8, center point at 81.4, and crease at 55.9. A raw lower crease percentage does not make the crease his weakest relative area.

**G4 — Damage mechanism**

Luke's 2025-26 tracking contains 49 midrange shots and five goals, 22 high-slot attempts, and 68 long-range shots with one goal. His inside activation is distinguished from his ordinary point release. Hischier, Jack Hughes, Gritsyuk and Hamilton retain independent shooting routes.

**G5 — Exact matchup alignment**

Luke's September 21 shots included direct releases from approximately 31.8 and 34.8 feet; on September 26 he again released from approximately 29.2 feet. These central/inside releases fit Woll's specific high-slot weakness. The selection does not rely on Woll allowing a long-range point shot or on a teammate's tip.

**G6 — Opportunity / access**

Projected first defensive pair and PP1 give Luke access to both even-strength activation and the man advantage. PP2 Hamilton and Gritsyuk, non-PP finishers and the fourth line remain eligible. The unit list is a dated projection, not a claim every skater assignment was independently confirmed.

**G7 — Recurrence / sequence**

Actual preseason appearances: September 21, three shots; September 26, one shot. Three of those four recorded attempts were the inside releases described above. This is a two-appearance spatial sequence, not five current games or a season total presented as recent form.

**G8 — Market fingerprint**

Dated previews mostly foreground Jack Hughes, Hischier and Meier. A historical Luke price is recoverable, but different archives show +500 and +600 without a verified movement timeline. Those are separate book/snapshot observations, not evidence of steam and not a reason to promote or reject Luke.

**G9 — Player × environment**

A defenseman entering the high slot fits this particular goalie map better than assuming every New Jersey point shot is favorable. Jack's carrying role and Nico's close finishing remain live challenges; identity and reputation do not select the recipient.

**G10 — Event route**

The evaluated route is possession or a return feed into Luke's inside step, followed by his own wrist/snap release from the high slot. Jack/Bratt creation, a Hamilton point attempt, and a net-front tip are different event routes. Only Luke's own credited finish satisfies this selected route.

**G10.5 — Recipient separation**

Primary considered: Jack Hughes. Challengers: Luke Hughes, Hischier, Gritsyuk, Hamilton, Evangelista, Meier, Bratt and the remaining eligible pool. Decision: advance Luke's repeated inside release for the selected high-slot attack. Nico's current closer finishes and Jack's mixed drive/circle routes are not silently treated as identical to that attack.

**G11 — Continuation**

Luke repeated the inside release in both of his last two preseason appearances and retained projected first-pair/PP access. This establishes continuation of the location/role, not a prediction that a prior goal must repeat; he had not scored in those two appearances.

**G12 — Event ownership**

Luke owns the shot only if he takes the inside release. Jack and Bratt can create the possession without inheriting its goal. A tip by a forward would belong to that forward. This is the final comparative recipient decision for this replay, not proof that Luke owns every New Jersey chance.

**G13 — Numerology / alignment**

Review uses October 1, jersey 43, birth date September 9, 2003, the Hughes family/name overlap and pregame career totals of 223 games, 23 goals and 128 points. The shared surname applies to both brothers and does not separate them. Hischier's 199 career goals put 200 one goal away; Meier is two games from 700. Those genuine milestone challenges are retained, but they do not replace the exact lane decision. No gematria total, numerical weight or new formula is invented.

**G14 — WHO / anomaly**

The anomaly is a defenseman repeatedly moving inside against a goalie whose center-point stopping was strong but high-slot stopping was weak. His current shot sequence and projected access independently support this path. It is not a WHO designation merely because he is a defenseman.

**G15 — Finisher protection**

Projected support includes Jack Hughes, Bratt and the PP forwards; Pesce supplies defensive-pair support. Their presence can sustain Luke's activation without being counted as his own scoring evidence. Receiving forwards and possible tip owners remain explicit competing routes.

**G16 — Final competition**

Luke survives the exact high-slot continuation comparison. Hischier's milestone and high-slot season history are serious counterevidence; Gritsyuk's midrange goal and Hamilton's inside activation also remain documented. The decision favors the repeated current release in the selected lane; no odds rank or invented numerical score resolves it.

**G17 — Contradiction audit**

Woll and Allen have dated pregame starter confirmations. Skater/PP information remains projected where no independent confirmation was recovered. Current team labels cannot remove Mercer from the October 1 pool. Luke's past majority point-shot profile and Nico's 200-goal milestone are preserved counterevidence. The replay is retrospective and was exposed to some results during research; it is not an original or blind pregame card.

**G18 — Final lock**

Historical projected-input replay selection: Luke Hughes, WHO path, PHI @ NJD. This name remains fixed for the scorer and mechanism grade.

### MIN @ NSH — Joel Eriksson Ek

**G0 — Game / goalie target**

Minnesota attacks Juuse Saros. His 2025-26 crease and low-slot stopping create the target; Wallstedt's stronger broad record is counterevidence against switching the attack side to Nashville.

**G1 — Environment**

Both teams are opening their season. Nashville conceded six goals in each of its last two preseason games. Minnesota's loss of Faber remains a defensive/territorial counterweight rather than being repurposed as evidence that a particular Wild forward scores.

**G2 — Full player pool**

All 18 projected Wild skaters are retained, including Brink, the Folignos, McCarron, Hunt and every defenseman. Hunt is resolved by his player ID and the dated Minnesota lineup, not removed because the current roster later places him in Nashville.

**G3 — Opponent attack map**

Saros's crease save rate was .673 and low slot .788, both at the 15.3rd NHL percentile. His high slot was .895 at the 91.5th percentile and both circles were comparatively strong. This is an inside receiving/rebound attack, not a general invitation for perimeter shooters.

**G4 — Damage mechanism**

Eriksson Ek's tracking shows 80 high-danger shots and 13 goals, including 11 crease and 69 low-slot attempts. Boldy had 84 high-danger shots/22 goals; Coleman 73/13; Brink 43/10; Hartman 59/19; McCarron had 15 crease attempts. Every one is a real competing interior route.

**G5 — Exact matchup alignment**

Eriksson Ek's crease/low-slot receiving mechanism fits the actual weak areas. His September 25 goal was a four-foot finish. Boldy's mixed perimeter/inside shot profile is not automatically converted into the same crease event; Brink and McCarron's closer routes remain genuine challenges.

**G6 — Opportunity / access**

Eriksson Ek is projected with Coleman and Boldy at even strength and on PP1. Coleman and the other PP2/depth finishers stay eligible. The even-strength trio and each PP unit are separate access states; a feed observed in one state does not confirm the same feed in another.

**G7 — Recurrence / sequence**

Last five recorded playoff appearances: April 30/28/25/22/20, shots 3/4/4/4/4, with one goal on April 22. His September 25 preseason appearance produced one shot and one goal. These are dated but separated samples. That lone current appearance must not be described as a repeated current receiving sequence.

**G8 — Market fingerprint**

A dated preview listed Eriksson Ek +210, Kaprizov +140 and Boldy +170; a separate Boldy page listed +190. These are book/snapshot differences, not authenticated movement. No price or star rank settles the interior-recipient comparison.

**G9 — Player × environment**

The intended attack is sustained inside access against Saros. Eriksson Ek occupies that area and can receive while other players carry or shoot from farther out. PP time supplies access; it does not establish who finishes a particular rebound.

**G10 — Event route**

The actual pregame example is the September 25 even-strength sequence: Coleman primary assist, Boldy secondary assist, Eriksson Ek finishing from four feet. A projected PP1 net-front route is a different branch whose creator chain is not proven by that even-strength event.

**G10.5 — Recipient separation**

Primary considered: Boldy. Challengers: Eriksson Ek, Coleman, Brink, Kaprizov, Hartman, McCarron and the full remaining pool. Decision: transfer the selected crease-receiving route to Eriksson Ek. Coleman's independent PP2 receiving route and Brink's current finishing evidence remain substantive challenges, not erased as mere support.

**G11 — Continuation**

The historical close-shot pattern and current four-foot goal support the receiving identity. Current sequence depth is limited to one preseason appearance for Eriksson Ek. This is the weakest continuation/separation part of this choice and remains visible through final competition.

**G12 — Event ownership**

Eriksson Ek is chosen for the evaluated crease branch, not declared owner of every Wild interior chance. Coleman is a creator in the cited even-strength event but can be a finisher on PP2; Brink can finish or distribute independently. Those roles must remain state-specific.

**G13 — Numerology / alignment**

Review uses October 1 opener, jersey 14, birth date January 29, 1997, and pregame totals of 614 games, 155 goals and 350 points. The 350-point mark was already reached; it is not a milestone scored on this night. Boldy has 144 career goals, Kaprizov is three games from 400, Brink is two points from 100 and Coleman seven games from 700. None is manufactured into a formula or used to skip the physical recipient comparison.

**G14 — WHO / anomaly**

Brink's new-team opportunity and two September 21 goals provide a real challenger anomaly, backed by current attempts and an inside route. McCarron's and the fourth line's crease access stay visible. No WHO quota forces one of them into the card.

**G15 — Finisher protection**

Coleman and Boldy provide projected even-strength support; Quinn Hughes and the other PP1 players provide a separate support branch. Support players retain their own scoring paths. Passing evidence cannot reduce them permanently to facilitators.

**G16 — Final competition**

Eriksson Ek remains the replay choice for the precise crease-receiving branch. The decision does not demonstrate unique overall event ownership: Brink's additional current appearance and Coleman's independent PP2 route are unresolved competitive strength, not missing player-pool entries.

**G17 — Contradiction audit**

Saros was confirmed in a dated morning report; Wallstedt later had a pregame confirmation. Skater/PP projections remain identified as such. The long gap before the single current preseason finish, strong competing interior players and the separation of EV from PP evidence remain explicit contradictions. No glove/blocker/five-hole claim is inferred from a rink-location map.

**G18 — Final lock**

Historical projected-input replay selection: Joel Eriksson Ek, TRANSFER path, MIN @ NSH. The subsequent grade does not replace him with whichever teammate scored.

### EDM @ VAN — Evan Bouchard

**G0 — Game / goalie target**

Edmonton attacks Kevin Lankinen. His weak high slot, low slot and right circle provide specific targets. Levi's limited prior NHL tracking is not converted into an invented Vancouver attack advantage.

**G1 — Environment**

The teams meet again after September 29. Edmonton generated 37 shots in that prior meeting. The prior 6-5 game is context available before October 1, not the October 1 outcome. Missing Hyman and Nugent-Hopkins change opportunity distribution.

**G2 — Full player pool**

All 18 projected Oilers are retained. Samanski's dated recall and original lineup outweigh his absence from a current roster response. Michaels and Formenton use real current preseason/opener evidence where 2025-26 NHL tracking is unavailable. Hyman and Nugent-Hopkins are not inserted from stale markets.

**G3 — Opponent attack map**

Lankinen high slot .785 (5.1st percentile), low slot .784 (10.2), right circle .851 (10.2), center point .937 (20.3). His left-circle and crease percentiles are much stronger. A blanket claim that the entire crease is weak would be incorrect.

**G4 — Damage mechanism**

Bouchard's prior tracking contains 87 midrange shots/12 goals and 108 long-range shots/four goals, including 34 high-slot attempts. His current three-goal opener included direct finishes from approximately 53.8, 17.0 and 59.5 feet. Point shooting and inside activation are recorded separately.

**G5 — Exact matchup alignment**

Bouchard has an evidenced central release and a separate inside step against the same target goalie. Both differ from crediting him for a forward's deflection. Podkolzin's low-slot/circle receiving and Draisaitl's inside/right-side shooting are direct rival routes; they are not excluded by reputation or unit status.

**G6 — Opportunity / access**

Projected Ekholm pairing and PP1 preserve Bouchard's access. Podkolzin's increased top-line/PP opportunity with Hyman out is material. Kapanen, PP2 players and depth scorers remain eligible. Empty-net situations and extra-attacker states are distinguished from ordinary 5-on-4 opportunity.

**G7 — Recurrence / sequence**

Actual recent shots, oldest to newest: September 19 three, September 26 four, September 29 six. Bouchard had three goals in the last meeting, but recurrence is documented by attempts and distinct routes rather than simply labeling him hot. His older playoff sequence supplies counterevidence to assuming conversion stays elevated.

**G8 — Market fingerprint**

A dated Bouchard page listed +310. Other dated markets foreground Draisaitl, McDavid and Podkolzin. One table still listed injured Hyman, so it cannot define the eligible pool. No book price, invented probability or unverified line move determines this choice.

**G9 — Player × environment**

Edmonton's pressure and repeated receiving/release opportunities fit the goalie target. A blue-line player can be the shot owner; the forwards' point totals do not automatically own his event. Conversely his assists on forward goals would not satisfy his scorer selection.

**G10 — Event route**

Evaluated branches are Bouchard's direct release through the central shooting lane and his documented inside activation. McDavid supplied two assists on Bouchard's September 29 goals; one was unassisted. A rebound off a defender or a boards bounce is not pre-credited as an exact match to those direct-shot branches.

**G10.5 — Recipient separation**

Primary: Bouchard. Challengers: Podkolzin, Draisaitl, McDavid, Kapanen, Shea and the full remaining pool. Decision: keep Bouchard's own direct-release/activation routes. Podkolzin's seven-shot, two-goal opener is a substantial receiving challenge; Shea's prior preseason inside finish is also retained.

**G11 — Continuation**

Shots appeared in all three recent appearances and projected deployment preserves access. There is no rule that a three-goal game predicts another goal. The 2025-26 and postseason records remain counterevidence to extrapolating the latest conversion rate.

**G12 — Event ownership**

Bouchard owns an untipped goal on his evaluated release or activation. A Podkolzin tip/rebound is Podkolzin's goal. The final decision does not silently reassign a forward's finish to the defenseman who created the shot.

**G13 — Numerology / alignment**

Review uses October 1, jersey 2 in Edmonton's second game, October 20 birth date, and pregame totals of 430 games, 79 goals and 338 points. One goal would reach the round personal total of 80. Podkolzin is two points from 100; Draisaitl's pregame totals are separately checked. These are qualitative calendar/number/milestone observations, not an invented gematria sum or weighted score.

**G14 — WHO / anomaly**

A defenseman's hat trick and inside activation are a real anomaly with documented opportunity. Podkolzin's changed receiving role and Shea's activation remain competing anomalies. The anomaly supports investigating the route rather than supplying an automatic promotion.

**G15 — Finisher protection**

McDavid, Draisaitl and the projected forwards can sustain possession and feed Bouchard; Ekholm supports the pairing. Every one retains an independent finishing route. Screens and tips are explicitly audited for scorer ownership.

**G16 — Final competition**

Bouchard remains the replay choice after the location, attempt continuation and direct credited receiving/release comparison. Podkolzin is the strongest unresolved forward challenge. No numerical rank, star tiebreak or price overrides the documented chain.

**G17 — Contradiction audit**

The starter archives have dated pregame reports; the Lankinen warmup confirmation precedes this game's start. His crease and left-circle strength are preserved. Dynamic current-season statistics on dated archive pages are excluded. The initial Hat-trick label does not collapse three different goal locations into one proven repeating mechanism.

**G18 — Final lock**

Historical projected-input replay selection: Evan Bouchard, PRIMARY path, EDM @ VAN. Scorer outcome and exact finishing-mechanism outcome are graded separately.

## Grade and mechanism audit

**Luke Hughes — scorer hit, mechanism match.** His own 33.2-foot snap release came from the selected high-slot route. This supports the location/recipient read for that event; it does not retroactively validate unrelated gates or all of New Jersey's goals.

**Evan Bouchard — scorer hit, mechanism not validated.** Boards rebound, Willander skate. The credited goal is kept as a hit, while the predicted direct-release route receives no automatic mechanism credit. The play event also lacks a shot-type field; the NHL narrative resolves why a goal coordinate alone is insufficient.

**Joel Eriksson Ek — scorer miss.** He had three shots and 5:44 of power-play time. Those shots came from approximately 18.0–23.1 feet, not the four-foot finish used as the closest current receiving example. Coleman finished a six-foot PP2 wrist-shot event with Brink and Yurov assisting. The target side/inside weakness was relevant, but the chosen recipient did not own that goal.

The Minnesota audit focus is the unsupported step from one verified even-strength Coleman/Boldy-to-Eriksson-Ek finish to a preferred overall crease recipient. The EV and PP1 creator/receiver chains are different. Coleman already had an independent projected PP2 route and must not be permanently reduced to a facilitator. The actual PP2 use of Brink is an additional deployment discrepancy against the captured projected-unit list; its availability before puck drop is not reconstructed from the postgame event.

Eriksson Ek stays a miss. Coleman is not substituted into the graded card. The result stays 2/3.

## Patches carried forward within the existing gates

| Existing gate | Execution correction | Applied consequence |
|---|---|---|
| G0 / G3 / G5 | Inspect the actual area inside a broad danger band; compare the goalie's relative area performance, not raw crease-versus-circle percentages | Woll's high-slot pocket changes the target comparison, and Luke's inside release is evaluated separately from his point shots |
| G6 | Preserve strength state and the actual unit behind each chance | EV, PP1, PP2, PK and extra-attacker access remain separate; an EV pass chain is not proof of a PP pass chain |
| G10 / G10.5 / G12 | Separate creator and receiver within each state; preserve a player's independent finishing role in other states | Coleman may create for Eriksson Ek at EV and finish on PP2. Both roles remain in the final competition |
| G7 / G11 | Distinguish one current appearance, multiple current appearances, old playoff sequence and current conversion | One preseason finish is not described as a repeated current ownership sequence |
| G17 | Keep dated status and later deployment changes distinct | Current roster labels do not remove historical eligible players; postgame PP use is not passed off as confirmed pregame information |
| G18 / postgame grade | Grade the credited scorer separately from the actual finishing mechanism | Bouchard remains a scorer hit with a mechanism mismatch; Luke remains a scorer and mechanism hit |

Shootout events are excluded from live-play sequences. Empty-net goals remain historical scoring events but are not evidence of exploiting a goalie. A 6-on-4 receiving event is not mislabeled ordinary 5-on-4. These are execution corrections, not additional gates, numerical weights, mandatory thresholds or a new G13 formula.

G13 remains a full qualitative name/date/number/alignment and milestone review, followed by G14–G18. Milestones already achieved are not announced as tonight's targets. WHO requires a documented anomaly, a physical path and actual access.

## G10.5 teammate challenges
PIT: Novak and Robertson preserve authentic midrange paths; Malkin on PP2 remains eligible; Rakell high-danger is not a primary matching lane vs Vladar stronger HD. Exact Chinakhov tip was *not* justified by MID goal-zone alone.
NYI: Heineman carries matching MID and potential shorthanded access but uneven 5-game shot recurrence; Horvat L1 PP1 history does not prove G12. Do not post-hoc elevate Heineman because he scored.
COL: Nelson is a serious PP2 high-danger challenger; MacKinnon and Necas on PP1 have separate damage routes. Lehkonen source case uniquely combines projected L1 + PP2 high danger, but Nelson challenge not mathematically or manually overridden.

## Postgame-only audit (never fed into pregame record)

Pittsburgh 7–0 Philadelphia: Chinakhov tipped Erik Karlsson point shot on PP. A scorer hit with invalidated midrange-specific damage mechanism; Malkin scored PP2.
Islanders 1–2 Toronto: Horvat no goals and one assist on Heineman’s SH finish; Cowan scored on PP2 and Sissons on a tip.
Los Angeles 4–8 Colorado: Lehkonen two PP slot goals vs Kuemper; Nelson scored once on PP2. Colorado PP 4/5 (team records may report 4/5 or 4/6 depending on simultaneous-penalty opportunities; official COL report gives 4/5).

## Patch decisions — no fabricated Blender rules

1. **Separate shooter zone from scoring event type:** tips, redirections, one-timers, rebounds and direct shots are not interchangeable at G3–G5/G10.
2. **Preserve penalty-kill challengers:** retrieve PK deployment and short-handed shot sequences before resolving G10.5/G12, not from final goals.
3. **Never equate PP2 with eliminated:** Lehkonen and Nelson both had direct high-danger opportunities against Kuemper PK.
4. **Distinguish game state for goalie weakness:** Kuemper PK .761 HD vs 5v5 .846 HD, Vladar MID .867 vs strong HD .822.
5. **Keep G13 qualitative and nonblocking; never create a formula or use it to stop G14–G18.**
6. **Keep original G18 unverified for all skaters until actual original timestamped selections and complete evidence exist.**

## Completion/accounting and limits
- **Completed now:** frozen 108-player pool preserved; full 20-gate detailed research journal retained; three independent 20-gate newly adjudicated trial candidates; full teammate research retained; three retrospective candidates and postgame scorer + mechanism grades; 7 concrete execution patches.
- **Not completed:** original historical G18 locks for this slate; 108 independently resolved G7–G12/G17 statuses (many still SEARCH); G1 independently labeled GREEN/NEUTRAL/RED; exact G12 ownership; the seven other historical slates’ verified G18 Core 3 grades.
- **Why the distinction matters:** A shadow trial created after game day is not a true blind pregame record and must not be advertised as one. Its outcome is a diagnostic only.

## Original sources and cutoff control
The included source register labels every item PREGAME or POSTGAME. No Sep30 scoring result is written into the PRE_PUCK_SHADOW_CANDIDATES file. All numeric goalie and NHL EDGE profiles are 2025–26 prior-season data available before Sep30.

Built 2026-11-05. No software app development or betting market ranking used.

## Concrete G17–G18 process corrections — no new gates

1. Preserve **all** dated selection versions, even when a later reply offers a replacement. Log FIRST / REPLACEMENT / CONFLICTING / LATE; do not overwrite.
2. Check actual **publication UTC converted to America/New_York** against earliest official start for **every** selected game. Score a late pick for accountability but never certify a fully pregame lock.
3. Use official **goal scorers**, not assists, shots, goalie saves, or another date. Correct the October 3 2/3 error to 0/3.
4. Keep scorer outcome and **mechanism outcome** independent. A player goal cannot certify G3→G5, G10 or G12 retroactively.
5. Contradictory claimed Core 3 or G18 lists on the **same slate** must be escalated at G17. They cannot all represent one deterministic final lock.
6. G13 continues through G14–G18 normally. No formula requirement or novel weight was introduced.

## Scope limit

The original eight selected research slates remain Sep 29, Sep 30, Oct 1, Oct 13, Oct 24, Nov 1, Nov 2, Nov 3. The extra October 2, 3, 5, 7 batches are supplemental **previously delivered** selections recovered during this continuation, not substitutions into the eight-slate study. The previous 46,080 gate journal entries do not establish G18 locks. This correction does not assert a completed 2,304-player gate replay.

## Completion verification

The five new selected-side pools were checked against their original dated player lists. Every replay uses three distinct games, separate G0–G18/G10.5 decisions, pre-date current-game data, an explicit fixed card and an official final scorer grade. Every selected target goalie ID matches the actual starting goalie; backup and empty-net goals remain distinct. Grade arithmetic, player IDs, alias handling, date boundaries, pool completeness and all source-time conversions were checked.

This completion does not claim an original historical publication was recovered where none exists, a blind backtest was conducted, or every predicted mechanism occurred. It completes the requested continuation of the grade–audit–patch work and carries its concrete execution corrections forward. No new app, empty research journal or replacement betting card is delivered.
